### Celotex Corp. v. Edwards, No. 93-1504. — 1995-04-19

Authority: [Record](../records/Celotex_Corp_v_Edwards_merits_1995-04-19.md#public-projection).

Chief Justice Stone-Zsela concurs in part and in the judgment. He joins the June-coverage and execution/review-remedy portions. The injunction had at least a colorable basis in the relationship between the bond, surety arrangements, and reorganization, and was not transparently void. Parties subject to such an operative order must challenge it in the issuing proceeding and through authorized review; they may not obtain contrary execution from another court. He therefore reverses and would set aside the execution authorization while leaving proper relief available. He does not join the Court's affirmative actual-relatedness or sufficient interim §157 determinations, or their complete collateral-review rationale. His ground neither protects an injunction lacking even an arguable jurisdictional basis nor decides ultimate statutory power to restrain every nondebtor surety. It supplies no contempt finding, perpetual restraint, or automatic appeal; Celotex's continuation burden and the qualified review routes remain.

Justice Stevens, joined by Justice Ginsburg, dissents. They accept that the June 13 order expressly reaches the bond proceeding and that immediate execution is actually related to the Chapter 11 case under §1334(b)'s nonexclusive district-court jurisdiction. Their objection concerns the allocation of adjudicative power to the bankruptcy judge under §157 and Article III. Northbrook's undertaking matured independently of Celotex's tort debt. A related controversy and the issuing judge's core label do not, in their view, empower that non-Article III judge to prevent the Article III bond court from deciding the undertaking when §157 assigns final determination to the district court. Calling the restraint interim does not answer that structural objection.

They accept that ordinary legal error does not generally authorize disobedience and do not reject orderly review of orders issued with the requisite authority. They regard that antecedent condition as unsatisfied here and would affirm enforcement of Northbrook's bond. They do not rely on the original October wording alone, deny all related jurisdiction, presume consent or a proved refusal, find exact collateral depletion, adopt a blanket ban on every nondebtor injunction, or assert the injunction's indefinite validity. Their ground concerns this statutory allocation and this restraint. It supplies no controlling rule and changes no amount of the undertaking.

These complete published positions are Stone-Zsela's concurrence in part and in the judgment and Stevens's dissent joined by Ginsburg, on April 19, 1995. Their separate reasoning is noncontrolling; Stone's joins in the Court's coverage and remedy portions have the controlling scope stated above. No separate §1481 history theory, October-only scope position, automatic-stay holding, or hypothetical consent finding is published.

### McIntyre v. Ohio Elections Commission, No. 93-986 (current petitioner: executor of Margaret McIntyre's estate). — 1995-04-19

Authority: [Record](../records/McIntyre_v_Ohio_Elections_Commission_merits_1995-04-19.md#public-projection).

Justice Thomas concurs in the judgment. Talley's protection and the historical expressive tradition it recognizes support an individual's anonymous political pamphlet. The supplied justification cannot sustain a penalty imposed solely for missing identification on this ordinary leaflet. He does not adopt the Court's modern value-balancing and content-based-scrutiny methodology as his own ground. His agreement in this judgment therefore adds no vote to the Court's complete scrutiny explanation, and his writing does not invalidate every financing or organizational disclosure rule.

Justice Scalia dissents. Election-specific attribution is a limited accountability requirement that helps voters identify a communication's source and assists enforcement against electoral wrongdoing. Buckley's disclosure principles and Bellotti's source-information discussion support that safeguard in his view. Talley's general prohibition covering all anonymous handbills is distinguishable from Ohio's election category. He recognizes the expressive burden but regards the State's justification as sufficient here; he would sustain the fine. He preserves appropriate protection where a speaker establishes threats or reprisals, without finding that McIntyre experienced either. He does not endorse a general power to forbid anonymous discussion outside the election setting. These complete published separate positions are effective April 19, 1995 and noncontrolling. Ginsburg publishes no separate opinion; her reservation is part of the Court's limits. No unverified original-era historical assertion or state-statute survey is incorporated into either writing.

### Kyles v. Whitley, No. 93-7927. — 1995-04-19

Authority: [Record](../records/Kyles_v_Whitley_merits_1995-04-19.md#public-projection).

Justice Scalia, joined by Justices Kennedy and Thomas, dissents. The three accept cumulative reasonable-probability review and consider the police-held material. They find that the supported additional uses do not sufficiently weaken the surviving identifications, physical evidence and adverse credibility findings to undermine confidence in this verdict. They treat the jury’s knowledge of a framing theory as relevant but not conclusive, and Beanie’s absence as a limitation on some uses rather than a bar to every investigative use. Their disagreement concerns the combined application: they reject the majority’s assessment that the new corroboration and provenance challenges materially change the evidentiary picture. They do not demand proof of innocence, impose a materiality threshold on each item, or hold that mere legal sufficiency defeats Brady. Having found no material violation, they would affirm denial of this claim without adopting a second harmlessness ground. Their application is noncontrolling.

Justice Stevens joins the Court’s application of Bagley without withdrawing his earlier request-sensitive criticism of that standard. The Court does not reject that criticism or adopt a new restrictive pretrial-disclosure theory; no additional writing is filed here.

### Rubin v. Coors Brewing Co., No. 93-1631. — 1995-04-19

Authority: [Record](../records/Rubin_v_Coors_Brewing_Co_merits_1995-04-19.md#public-projection).

Justice Stevens concurs in the judgment. Accurate, unadorned information about a lawful product cannot be suppressed here simply because consumers may use it to choose a stronger beverage. His Edge dissent identifies the objection to keeping consumers uninformed, while Discovery Network requires the selected speech category to relate to an actual harm. He agrees that this scheme fails to advance the stated objective, but does not suggest that greater success at withholding truthful knowledge would cure the underlying objection. His Lanphere joinder in a bounded Central Hudson application did not abandon that distinct ground. He does not deny the Government’s authority to regulate alcohol’s harms directly, forbid deceptive claims, or require properly justified useful disclosures. He declines to join the Court’s commercial-speech framework, its acceptance of the information-suppression interest as framed, or its Fox alternative holding. His truthful-information rationale is noncontrolling; the eight-Justice Court opinion controls without combining separate grounds.

### Stone v. Immigration and Naturalization Service, No. 93-1199. — 1995-04-19

Authority: [Record](../records/Stone_v_Immigration_and_Naturalization_Service_merits_1995-04-19.md#public-projection).

Justice Breyer, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices O’Connor and Souter, dissents. Congress expressly incorporated the ordinary administrative-review procedure, and Locomotive Engineers treats a timely reconsideration motion filed before judicial review as postponing reviewable finality. Ibarra supports that treatment of an operative order still under timely reconsideration; Darby cautions against adding an unprovided procedural forfeiture. The dissent reads mandatory consolidation as coordination when an original judicial petition is already pending and review of reconsideration is later sought. It therefore gives subsection (a)(6) work without treating it as withdrawal of the incorporated pre-review rule. On that construction, the March 25 petition was timely after the February 3 denial. The four would restore review of the original order within the statutory channel. They would not revive an expired right through a late motion, grant an automatic administrative stay, impose optional reconsideration as exhaustion, decide an already pending court’s jurisdiction by agency choice, or award immigration relief. This construction and remedy are noncontrolling.

### California Department of Corrections v. Morales, No. 93-1462. — 1995-04-25

Authority: [Record](../records/California_Department_of_Corrections_v_Morales_merits_1995-04-25.md#public-projection).

Chief Justice Stone-Zsela fully joins the Court’s opinion and the opportunity-protecting position joined by Justice Stevens. He adds that when a State removes a legally guaranteed meaningful avenue to earlier release, a replacement must preserve the opportunity in substance. It must be legally available, practically accessible, timely enough to permit consideration before the postponed hearing while earlier release remains legally possible, and enforceable as a usable protection. Discretionary authority to advance a hearing does not, by itself, establish an adequate substitute. Weaver’s treatment of discretionary credits supports distinguishing an enforceable opportunity from officials’ abstract power to act. Lindsey and Miller explain why a possibly identical punishment under former law does not defeat the claim; Cavanaugh supplies the direct opportunity rule.

The prisoner must identify the former entitlement, its retrospective withdrawal and its concrete link to possible earlier release. The State must then substantiate any replacement it relies on, including legal basis, practical availability and timely capacity; theoretical authority requires evidence where operation matters. Uncertainty about that answer cannot justify denying otherwise established relief. Ultimate constitutional proof remains with the prisoner. He accepts the annual default, narrow class, permissive three-year maximum, separate findings, lawful factual overlap and initial review, but finds they do not preserve later rehabilitation, changed circumstances or correction of a materially mistaken forecast. A different arrangement may suffice if it actually preserves the opportunity through an adequate enforceable substitute; no hypothetical system, universal filing procedure or numerical deadline is approved. His full joinder remains intact; the broader formulation has no additional joins and is noncontrolling.

Justice Kennedy dissents, joined throughout by Justices Scalia, Thomas, and Breyer. They would reverse the hearing-frequency judgment and remand consistently with rejecting the claim. They accept Cavanaugh’s binding opportunity protection and absence of an actual-parole proof requirement, but give weight to its individualized-postponement reservation. California retains annual consideration unless a hearing produces a separate, reviewable prediction directed to the intervening years. Permission is restricted to multiple life-taking convictions, requires stated bases, permits a shorter interval and is capped at three years. Jackson requires separately directed reasons while allowing underlying facts to overlap suitability reasons. Morales’s continued-risk findings supply an additional legal decision absent from automatic deletion of alternate hearings.

They acknowledge that forecasts can become outdated and initial review is not later reconsideration. Their sufficient distinction relies on neither unproved ordinary reopening, a binding nineteen-year minimum, aggregate probabilities nor legal impossibility of earlier release. Collins’s punishment boundary and Cavanaugh’s reserved category support their conclusion; a procedural label alone would not suffice. They accept substantiation of an asserted substitute without making equivalent interim review indispensable to every justified delay. Kennedy accepts a bounded method of testing such assertions without joining the Court’s application; Scalia, Thomas and Breyer do not join an independently reusable enumerated allocation rule. Defective, perfunctory, categorical or materially different punitive implementation remains open. They grant no parole and leave rejected claims and later enactments untouched. Their distinction has four votes and does not control.


### United States v. Williams, No. 94-395. — 1995-04-25

Authority: [Record](../records/United_States_v_Williams_merits_1995-04-25.md#public-projection).

Justice Scalia concurs, joining the Court's express-waiver and connected timing analysis and its treatment of the distinct third-party remedies. Nordic Village requires clarity in the scope as well as the existence of a monetary waiver. Here the express wrongful-collection language supplies that consent, and the remote timing provision cannot plausibly impose the significant implicit claimant exclusion urged by the Government. He withholds the independently sufficient conclusion that Williams is a taxpayer under §§6511 and 7701 because that alternative is unnecessary to his disposition. He expresses no contrary view on that definition. Ordinary administrative-claim and timing requirements remain effective, and he joins no equitable exception to sovereign immunity.

Chief Justice Stone-Zsela concurs in the Court's primary ground and judgment. The collection was directed against Williams through a lien on property she claimed, and her payment therefore gives her a concrete collection injury even though another person was assessed. The levy, quiet-title, and substitute-fund mechanisms did not provide an adequate route in these circumstances: no levy occurred, quiet title did not furnish a realistic postpayment recovery, and a discretionary discharge requiring an agreement was not an assured substitute for refund review. He treats that remedial setting as confirmation of the enacted waiver's application, not as an equitable enlargement of consent. His ground remains confined to a compelled payment connected with the claimant's property, leaving volunteers and strangers outside the holding. He does not adopt the separate taxpayer-definition alternative. The refund suit proceeds subject to the administrative claim, merits, and proof requirements, without a present monetary award. His additional explanation does not create a general adequacy-of-remedy test.

Justice Kennedy, joined by Justice Thomas, dissents. Dalm requires the broad refund language to be read with the duly filed claim requirement, the timing provision's taxpayer language, and §7701's definition. In their view those connected provisions do not unequivocally authorize a person concededly not liable for the tax to invoke the taxpayer refund action. Wrongful collection and personal liability are distinct, but collection against claimed property does not itself make that person the taxpayer Congress identified. Nordic Village requires clear consent for the requested remedy and its scope; inconvenience cannot supply it. The specially drawn third-party statutes reinforce that reading without proving that any alternative actually was available to Williams or had to be exhausted. The dissent does not call a lien a levy, treat a permitted substitute-fund agreement as mandatory, or decide lien validity. Stahmann involved a statute intended to burden the producers and an action against the collector; it does not settle this different waiver. The two would reverse the jurisdictional judgment and restore dismissal of this refund action, leaving other lawfully authorized remedies and the merits unadjudicated. Their claimant construction is noncontrolling.

### New York State Conference of Blue Cross & Blue Shield Plans v. Travelers Insurance Co.; Pataki v. Travelers Insurance Co.; Hospital Association of New York State v. Travelers Insurance Co., Nos. 93-1408, 93-1414 and 93-1415. — 1995-04-26

Authority: [Record](../records/New_York_State_Conference_of_Blue_Cross_Blue_Shield_Plans_v_Travelers_Insurance_Co_merits_1995-04-26.md#public-projection).

All nine Justices join the Court’s opinion; no separate writing is filed.

### United States v. Lopez, No. 93-1260. — 1995-04-26

Authority: [Record](../records/United_States_v_Lopez_merits_1995-04-26.md#public-projection).

Chief Justice Stone-Zsela joins the Court’s limited holdings and concurs. Federal commerce authority is broad but enumerated. His complete account permits regulation of: actual interstate transactions; channels of commerce; instrumentalities, persons and things in interstate commerce; economic classes substantially affecting an actual interstate market; intrastate conduct whose inclusion is necessary to prevent evasion or material impairment of a valid interstate regime; and materially established interstate externalities that individual States cannot internalize. Each route requires its relevant connection to be established. A jurisdictional phrase must perform genuine constitutional work in the individual case. No other Justice joins this complete formulation.

He rejects the chain from guns near schools through violence or fear, impaired education, reduced productivity and the national economy because it supplies no judicially enforceable stopping point. National importance and generalized downstream social costs do not establish the missing enumerated-power connection. The defect is not the word noneconomic: a valid interstate regime or genuinely demonstrated interstate spillovers can support regulation of intrastate conduct under his account. Wickard and Perez remain at their actual economic-class scope. He would not insert a missing element, adjudicate an amended law, or foreclose a materially different enactment supported by its own constitutional authority. Commerce authority and individual rights are distinct inquiries; ordinary state police authority does not settle compliance with an independently applicable right. His concurrence establishes no Second Amendment classification and preserves the Elections Clause and Reconstruction Amendments’ own texts and standards. The additional framework is noncontrolling.

Justice Breyer, joined by Justices Stevens, Souter and Ginsburg, dissents. Congress could rationally find that the class of school gun possession substantially affects national commerce through disrupted education, workforce preparation and travel. Wickard and Perez require attention to the class rather than treating one defendant’s effect as independently substantial. The dissent would retain judicial review, a substantial class relationship and reasonable means, while deferring to the legislative judgment presented here. It rejects a universal findings prerequisite and does not equate every imaginable consequence with a substantial commercial effect. In its view, the Court discounts the practical national connection by treating the local and noncommercial features of possession as insufficiently connected to the class-wide effects. Safe schools’ desirability alone and the gun’s remote interstate manufacture are not its grounds. The dissent reads the original offense without a new nexus element; on that reading the indictment omits no required commerce allegation. It would reverse the constitutional dismissal and remand for the unresolved supervised-release and special-assessment objections, not approve the entire sentence. Its application is noncontrolling.

### United States v. Harris, No. 94-297. — 1995-04-27

Authority: [Record](../records/United_States_v_Harris_merits_1995-04-27.md#public-projection).

Chief Justice Stone-Zsela concurs in part and in the judgment. On commerce, he does not join the Court's economic-class rationale. He would sustain this prosecution because Congress may protect an interstate transportation instrumentality from a direct intrastate attack when the offense requires proof of that particular instrumentality's interstate connection. An automobile can carry persons and property through interstate channels; these defendants forcibly seized possession and control of the Buick itself. Its Michigan manufacture and Arkansas and Mississippi titling satisfied the enacted connection. The direct taking and vehicle-specific element together perform constitutional work, rather than attaching remote history to an unrelated local offense. He rejects the Fifth Circuit's general chain through travel, insurance, violent-crime costs, and the national economy. His ground neither reclassifies violent crime as economic activity nor makes every offense in or near a vehicle federal. Past movement alone is not a universal source of authority. This instrumentality application has no additional joins.

On fault and notice, the Chief Justice joins the knowing-participation and fair-notice propositions, requiring proof of the firearm and interstate-vehicle facts as enacted. He additionally concludes that this original statute requires neither knowledge that the vehicle traveled interstate nor a separate intent permanently to deprive. Ratzlaf's distinct willfulness language supplies no such requirements, and Staples's concern with apparently innocent possession turning on an unknown physical characteristic differs from pointing a shotgun at occupants and ordering them out. He finds the jury evidence sufficient to establish knowing participation beyond a reasonable doubt. That explanation does not convert the Court's bounded statute-level ruling into a separate majority adjudication of evidentiary sufficiency or of an unknown instruction. He does not eliminate fault from a fact Congress makes part of the wrongful conduct. His additional nexus-knowledge and deprivation-duration conclusions are noncontrolling. He joins the statutory authorization and bounded Hunter application sustaining the consecutive punishment and reserves a different pair lacking clear authorization.

Justice Stevens concurs. He joins the Court's statutory construction and its limited application of Hunter as governing precedent. He does not withdraw his Hunter dissenting position: multiple convictions can create stigma, risks, and collateral consequences distinct from the total authorized prison term, and his earlier view would give the same-offense protection content independent of legislative authorization. The present opinion neither denies those consequences nor announces unlimited legislative power to multiply convictions. Its application of existing precedent to this clear cumulative command permits his join without deciding again the constitutional foundation on which he previously disagreed. He also joins the Court's economic vehicle-taking ground without withdrawing his broader Lopez dissenting position. His preserved constitutional objection remains noncontrolling; he supports affirmance of both convictions and the consecutive terms here.

### United States v. Pinson, No. 94-164; Eighth Circuit No. 93-2851. — 1995-05-08

Authority: [Record](../records/United_States_v_Pinson_merits_1995-05-08.md#public-projection).

**Stone-Zsela, concurring in part and in the judgment.** The Chief joins Parts I and III: this examination is a search, and the challenged judgment must be vacated with the stated preservation-sensitive remand. He would state the home-information rule more generally: law-enforcement use of sense-enhancing technology from outside a home to obtain information about activity within that ordinary public observation could not otherwise obtain constitutes a Fourth Amendment search. His reasoning does not turn on whether the instrument receives reflected light, sound, heat, or another physical signal. The protection of the interior cannot end at the physical surface of its walls. Treating every measurable escaping emission as abandoned would permit increasingly capable instruments to obtain domestic information while preserving only formal protection against entry. The officers deliberately obtained heat-pattern information connected with concealed activity that their unaided senses could not discern; the absence of a conventional image does not change that conclusion.

The Chief does not classify ordinary visual observation from a lawful vantage, smoke or other conditions perceptible to unaided members of the public, utility records obtained through lawful process, or every technological measurement near private property as a search. His general rule retains its trigger: deliberate technological acquisition of otherwise unavailable information about activity inside a home. He expresses no view on excised-affidavit sufficiency, procedural availability of that issue, ultimate suppression, or Leon's application. His remand does not direct a new probable-cause determination. Stone-Zsela does not join Part II and expresses no view on its additional proposition. No other Justice joins his broader formulation.

**Kennedy, dissenting, joined by Breyer.** These Justices would affirm rejection of the search claim and the challenged conviction affirmance because the demonstrated output was comparative exterior warmth. Karo disclosed the presence of a particular object inside a home; this account does not show identification of a person, object, or exact interior activity. The causal inference officers drew from surface conditions does not, in their view, establish acquisition of the concealed fact Karo protected. They recognize that Katz and Karo make neither physical penetration nor the receiver's location dispositive. Their disagreement concerns the content obtained on this record, not immunity for all outside observation. Riley's exposed conditions and Dow's limited external depiction illustrate that distinction without establishing missing public-flight facts or converting a home into industrial premises.

They adopt no general heat-abandonment rule, contraband-only analogy, public-availability test, or technology good-faith defense. More revealing surveillance remains open. They need no alternative holding approving affidavit sufficiency or objective reliance. If the Court's search rule governs, they accept that any further proceedings must respect the same preservation limits; that conditional procedural agreement does not change their votes to affirm. They do not independently revisit the lesser-harms sentence.

### Hubbard v. United States, No. 94-172; Sixth Circuit No. 91-1775. — 1995-05-15

Authority: [Record](../records/Hubbard_v_United_States_merits_1995-05-15.md#public-projection).

**Stevens, concurring, joined by Ginsburg and Breyer.** These Justices join the Court's construction, full Bramblett treatment, and count-specific remedy. They supply an additional case-specific explanation for correcting the precedent. The judicial-function decisions have become an entrenched legal development attempting to protect adversarial proceedings from Bramblett's broad language. Those lower decisions do not authorize vertical disobedience or themselves overrule this Court. Their sustained development nevertheless deserves respect in assessing the consequences of the original construction. Correcting the entity boundary preserves their essential protection while dispensing with a function line the enacted language does not contain.

The statute's definitions and actual context supply the first-instance construction, but disagreement with Bramblett is not the entire precedent inquiry. Hilton and Allied-Bruce show why statutory precedent has institutional force and why settled arrangements matter. Here the combination of the particular definitional defect, the entrenched limiting development, and the qualified reliance record supports correction. The parties agree that prosecution guidance favored other charges in the described court-statement setting, but differ about its scope. That evidence weakens an assertion of indispensable, uniform reliance on litigation prosecutions; it proves neither total nonenforcement nor Hubbard's personal reliance. Congress's silence is not ratification. Crandon supports attention to criminal statutory setting and fair warning, without supplying a rule that every criminal precedent may be discarded.

The ordinary knowledge, falsity, and disclosure-duty requirements protect lawful advocacy but do not remove every cost of threatened prosecution or disputed classification. These Justices recognize that concern without finding an empirically established chilling effect or a right to lie. They adopt no independent lenity holding: genuine residual uncertainty after ordinary construction and precedent treatment is not an operative premise of this decision. Their complete explanation has three votes and is not the Court's general stare decisis standard.

**Scalia, concurring, joined by Kennedy.** These Justices also join the Court's construction, express overruling, and precise remedy. They reject treating the lower courts' limiting decisions as a competing body of authority entitled to displace this Court's statutory construction. Those cases instead demonstrate the serious litigation risks generated over time by the erroneous expansion and the absence of a rational repair grounded in the text. That combination supplies the reason beyond statutory error for overruling here. It would be incoherent to preserve Bramblett's legislative rationale by treating its judicial language as dispensable, because the same all-branch construction produced both.

Patterson requires a special justification; neither Kennedy's Hilton opinion nor his Allied-Bruce join imposes unconditional retention of an erroneous criminal construction. Scalia's Crandon concurrence, which Kennedy joined, takes cautious legal advice and inconsistent executive practice seriously but expressly distinguishes such practice from established judicial precedent. The additional justification here must therefore come from the demonstrated legal difficulties and consequences, not from treating the prosecutor's recommendation as law. The guidance remains qualified, and no assured restraint, universal nonenforcement, or personal reliance is found.

Ordinary offense elements reduce the risk to legitimate adversarial work without logically eliminating investigation and classification costs. Conversely, concern about litigation costs alone cannot invalidate an enacted crime. These Justices conclude that this erroneous construction can be retained only by irrational or textless limitations, and that the statutory boundary should be corrected directly. They do not adopt an independently sufficient lenity ground or a policy-made judicial-function exception. Their complete additional account has two votes. Thomas joins neither this framework nor the competing three-Justice account; his joins remain the Court's statutory construction and express precedent treatment, with practical concerns corroborative rather than independently controlling.

**Stone-Zsela, concurring in part and in the judgment.** The Chief joins the conclusion that the question presented may be reached and joins the count-specific mandate. He would overrule Bramblett only to the extent it treats an Article III court acting in its adjudicative capacity as a department or agency under §1001. He applies that rule to these adversarial bankruptcy filings and would vacate the resulting §1001 convictions. Section 151 identifies the bankruptcy court as a unit of the district court; that institutional relationship accompanies his application without a new classification of bankruptcy judges as Article III judges or of every Judicial Branch body as a court. His characterization of these filings is his legal application, not a finding that the circuit resolved the Government's administrative-asset-collection theory.

Patterson, Square D, and Illinois Brick give statutory stare decisis unusual force because Congress can amend the law. A different preferred first reading is insufficient. The required special justification is the combination present here: Bramblett's actual facts involved the House, its broad definition was extended to adjudicating courts despite Congress's express statutory usage, and decades of attempts to carve a judicial-function exception show that its broadest language cannot be administered without exceptions the text does not supply. Those lower decisions do not displace Bramblett by attrition. They demonstrate the unworkability of retaining its broadest formulation. A direct correction of the statutory boundary is more candid and administrable than continuing textless exceptions.

Concrete reliance on applying §1001 to adversarial court filings is slight, while perjury, contempt, bankruptcy fraud, obstruction, and other enacted offenses remain available on their own terms. The Chief creates no immunity for falsehoods to executive agencies merely because litigation is pending and does not affect statutes expressly covering court filings or sworn statements. Congress remains free expressly to extend a false-statement offense to all three branches and define safeguards and exceptions appropriate to adversarial proceedings. He supplies no sentence-wide relief and leaves unaffected counts and proceedings subject to the lower court's judgment. He does not join the associates' broader entity rule, their full displacement of Bramblett, or either complete extended stare decisis account. His narrower formulation has his vote alone.

**O’Connor, dissenting, joined by Souter.** These Justices join the Court's conclusion and complete analysis that the entity question is properly presented, but would affirm Counts V–VII. Section 6 expressly allows context to expand the ordinary department meaning. Bramblett considered that provision and the statute's history, and its construction has the institutional force of a statutory precedent Congress may revise. Their disagreement is not a denial of the force of the ordinary definition; it concerns whether the reasons now offered suffice to unsettle a construction already made. O’Connor's Allied-Bruce concurrence and Souter's Hilton and Allied-Bruce joins distinguish a preferred initial reading from the duty to treat statutory precedent seriously. Their Rowland dissent likewise gives actual context substantive work.

They acknowledge the prosecution guidance and the possible burden on legitimate advocacy. The recommendation is neither irrelevant nor binding, and its qualified scope does not establish abandonment of the statute. The difficulties of an added judicial-function exception do not demonstrate that the basic retained coverage rule is unworkable; refusing that extra exception can produce a coherent rule. Neither Congress's silence nor assured prosecutorial restraint supplies an additional ground.

On the retained construction, the proposed judicial-function and private-litigation exclusions lack an enacted basis. Rodgers supports authorized-function coverage but expressly reserved the judicial-function question; it did not already decide the exception's validity. Heintz is a limited analogy against adding a litigation category, not an answer to this criminal statute. The actual counts involve knowing factual falsehoods, not a not-guilty plea or a mere demand for proof. Knowledge and falsity requirements, and a disclosure duty for concealment, protect lawful conduct without eliminating every process cost. These Justices adopt no administrative classification of the filings, no universal legal-knowledge element, and no independent lenity ground. Their affirmance reaches only the three challenged convictions.

### Kansas v. Colorado, No. 105, Original. — 1995-05-15

Authority: [Record](../records/Kansas_v_Colorado_original_exceptions_1995-05-15.md#public-projection).

All nine Justices join the opinion of the Court and its remedy direction. No separate writing is filed.

### City of Edmonds v. Oxford House, Inc.; No. 94-23 - 1995-05-15

Authority: [Record](../records/City_of_Edmonds_v_Oxford_House_merits_1995-05-15.md#public-projection)

Published dissent preserves the category-specific numerical-cap view of Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas as noncontrolling continuity. No roster, circuit or institutional practice changes. No precedent is displaced for another identified case.

Thomas, joined by Scalia and Kennedy, concludes that the statutory words encompass a reasonable numerical restriction for an unrelated household even if related households follow another rule. The exception contains no express universal-ceiling requirement. Their position does not establish that every classification is reasonable or deny disability protection outside the exemption.

The appellate judgment stands; the case returns for remaining FHA proceedings. No future Supreme Court matter is left open by this merits event.

### Day v. Holahan; No. 94-672 - 1995-05-15

Authority: [Record](../records/Day_v_Holahan_merits_1995-05-15.md#public-projection)

Preserve all published noncontrolling positions: Kennedy/O'Connor/Scalia/Thomas targeted adverse-trigger burden; Stone's complete actual-operation responsive-financing framework at its qualified scope; Stevens/Souter/Ginsburg/Breyer no-cap/diversion and added-advocacy ground. Their differing reporting disposition depends on financing's validity. The Court's controlling reporting, ceiling and MCCL rules are limited exactly as above. No roster/allotment/practice changes or expressly displaced later-case predicate is established. MCCL's material future funding/activity changes permit reassessment, not automatic loss of protection.

Kennedy's plurality, joined by O'Connor, Scalia and Thomas, treats the programmed amount-linked adverse benefit as a legal burden on the independent speaker, even without a spending ceiling. Buckley and NCPAC protect independent advocacy; an ordinarily permissible voluntary grant differs from selecting adverse independent advocacy as the proportional trigger for enlarged spending authority and public funds. The plurality does not recognize a right to escape private rebuttal or prohibit ordinary public grants. It regards Minnesota's asserted participation justification as insufficient on the established record and rejects equalizing influence as a sufficient suppression rationale.

Stone-Zsela concurs in the financing judgment alone. He rejects both categorical rules: public funding of Speaker B ordinarily does not burden Speaker A, yet an independent-expenditure trigger is not automatically valid. His inquiry examines the actual legal burden, whether candidate participation is genuinely voluntary, whether the independent speaker remains legally free to spend, whether the response is bounded rather than indefinitely escalating, whether the State identifies a legitimate program objective beyond equalizing influence, and the demonstrated relationship between trigger and objective. The established over-$100 operational scenario increases an affected candidate's allowance and may yield a half-amount subsidy, subject to statutory eligibility and twice-minimum-match requirements. Participation was already about 96 percent in 1990 and 97 percent among legislative candidates in 1992; these different populations cannot be compared as a common measured trend. This record failed to substantiate the claimed need to preserve participation. Electoral-result eligibility also creates the implementation difficulty the lower opinion identified in relation to statutory payment commands; he does not invent an automatic immediate subsidy or new statutory primary-election exemption. The defect is this particular automatic, potentially escalating response's insufficient programmatic connection, not public funds answering private speech. His framework leaves room for benefits adjusted to genuine electoral circumstances, without financing primarily to neutralize disfavored advocacy. It has one vote and is noncontrolling.

His Court opinion confines special notice invalidity to its material function implementing the invalid mechanism, preserves ordinary informational/anticorruption/enforcement disclosure, and applies Minnesota's actual severability qualification. The contribution opinion rejects the lower court's categorical suggestion that intermediaries pose no corruption concern simply because they lack governmental power. It identifies legally permitted candidate relationships, realistic corruption or circumvention routes, practical magnitude and meaningful pooled advocacy as relevant inquiry subjects without a mandatory universal dollar minimum; the common holding is this ceiling's deficient fit. The nonprofit opinion preserves an objective safe harbor while allowing as-applied protection for an actual voluntary political association outside its literal conditions. Economic claimants, substantial commercial activity, material corporate or union funding and whether advocacy resources reflect voluntary political support are relevant. Minor incidental revenue or a missing formal policy alone is not conclusive; commercial financing or control can require different treatment. These applications do not overrule Austin.

Stevens, joined by Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer, dissents from financing and derivative reporting results. The independent speaker remains uncapped and retains its resources; a voluntary public program facilitates additional advocacy rather than confiscating speech. They reserve materially coercive or punitive applications and do not permit equalization as a basis for direct suppression. Because the trigger should survive, its notice mechanism cannot be invalidated solely as an orphaned implementation provision. They would remand preserved independent vagueness and overbreadth objections rather than affirm disclosure in all respects. They join the contribution and MCCL applications, preserving realistic intermediary corruption controls and regulation of materially different business wealth.

The Eighth Circuit judgment stands. The lower courts implement the separate permanent restraints and MCCL's as-applied protection. Ordinary reporting and materially changed nonprofit funding/operations require their own lawful assessment; no finding about future operations is made. The unreached notice vagueness/overbreadth theories need not be decided under this judgment.

### Reynoldsville Casket Co. v. Hyde; No. 94-3 - 1995-05-15

Authority: [Record](../records/Reynoldsville_Casket_Co_v_Hyde_merits_1995-05-15.md#public-projection)

Preserve Scalia/Thomas declaratory account and Kennedy/O'Connor independently justified equitable-remedy emphasis as published noncontrolling positions. No roster/practice changes. No automatic finality reopening or cross-case remedy entitlement.

Scalia, joined by Thomas, explains that judicial declaration of governing law supports retroactive application; that account is additional to the Court's narrow reliance rule. Kennedy, joined by O'Connor, agrees to reverse but stresses that reliance may matter under independently justified equitable rules. The judgment cannot stand on reliance alone; their concurrence refuses to treat application of the federal rule as abolition of every lawful reliance-sensitive remedy.

Remand to the Ohio courts for application of Bendix and remaining lawful issues. The action's ultimate timeliness and any actually supported independent ground remain for those proceedings.

### First Options of Chicago, Inc. v. Kaplan; No. 94-560. - 1995-05-22

Authority: [Record](../records/First_Options_of_Chicago_Inc_v_Kaplan_merits_1995-05-22.md#public-projection)

Courts independently determine agreement to arbitrate arbitrability unless ordinary applicable contract law establishes clear and unmistakable delegation. Presenting an objection while denying panel jurisdiction does not itself establish delegation; company submission does not itself establish an owner's personal assent. Where parties actually delegate arbitrability, limited award review governs that decision. Appellate courts independently review district-court legal conclusions and review factual findings for clear error. Arbitration supplies no additional appellate deference to the district judge's independent arbitrability determination.

Third Circuit personal-award judgment remains in force;corporate submission remains distinct. Supreme Court merits event is complete;no new Supreme Court step is created.

Published noncontrolling positions: No separate writing is filed. Stone-Zsela's partial join reserves merits-award deference,broad delegation clauses and incorporated rules without adopting an affirmative alternative or changing the judgment. No unpublished commitment changes current law.

### Kelley v. Board of Trustees of the University of Illinois; No. 94-783 (simulation-assigned). - 1995-05-22

Authority: [Record](../records/Kelley_v_Board_of_Trustees_of_the_University_of_Illinois_merits_1995-05-22.md#public-projection)

Title IX does not guarantee preservation of a particular varsity team or sport-for-sport counterparts. Lawful separate teams remain subject to equal opportunity; effective accommodation may be established through substantial proportionality, a history and continuing responsive expansion for the underrepresented sex, or full and effective accommodation of that sex's interests and abilities, with any one route sufficient and distinct regulatory requirements independently binding. A public university may consider sex during genuine fiscal or programmatic restructuring to preserve opportunities against demonstrated inequality only when the choice serves an actual important objective and is substantially related to it. Ratios, budget pressure or attempted federal compliance alone are insufficient; stereotypes, pretext and measures materially broader than the demonstrated need are forbidden.

Judgment for the University remains;Supreme Court merits event is complete. No reinstatement,damages or further remedy proceedings are ordered on rejected claims.

Published noncontrolling positions: No separate writing is filed. No unpublished commitment changes current law.

### U.S. Term Limits, Inc. v. Thornton / Bryant v. Hill; Nos. 93-1456, 93-1828 - 1995-05-22

Authority: [Record](../records/US_Term_Limits_v_Thornton_Bryant_v_Hill_merits_1995-05-22.md#public-projection)

Kennedy's separate national-representative structure and Thomas/O'Connor/Scalia retained-state-electoral-authority position are published noncontrolling continuity. No change to state-officer law, roster or circuit allocation. No broader right to Congress redesign or competitive election is created.

Kennedy emphasizes that national representatives derive their constitutional place from the people within the federal structure; States' authority over their own officers does not extend to altering that national representative relationship. He joins the common Article I holding. Thomas, joined by O'Connor and Scalia, reads Powell as limiting Congress, not the people's retained electoral authority within each State. He considers this ordinary-ballot limitation permissible while write-in eligibility remains. The dissent must defend the substantive service criterion as an exercise of retained authority, not merely invoke a procedural label.

The Arkansas judgment stands in both consolidated dockets. Section 3's congressional service-based ballot exclusions remain unenforceable; ordinary lawful state election administration continues. No further Supreme Court procedural event is created.

### Wilson v. Arkansas; No. 94-5707. - 1995-05-22

Authority: [Record](../records/Wilson_v_Arkansas_merits_1995-05-22.md#public-projection)

Common-law announcement of identity and authority forms part of Fourth Amendment reasonableness when officers enter a dwelling. Supported countervailing circumstances, including danger, futility, escape or threatened destruction of evidence, may justify unannounced entry; actual justification and remedy remain for the state court.

State-court proceedings resume on remand for actual reasonableness,excuses and remedy. Supreme Court merits event is complete;the ultimate conviction consequence remains for adjudication.

Published noncontrolling positions: No separate writing is filed. No unpublished commitment changes current law.

### Garlotte v. Fordice, No. 94-6790 - 1995-05-30

Authority: [Record](../records/Garlotte_v_Fordice_merits_1995-05-30.md#public-projection)

Thomas dissents without joins. He reads § 2254’s custody requirement as demanding custody under the conviction attacked, and treats the completed marijuana sentence as expired for that purpose. He confines Peyton’s aggregation to a still-unserved sentence, where advance review avoids waiting for its commencement. That purpose, in his view, does not permit reopening an already completed segment merely because success could accelerate eventual release. He invokes Maleng’s limit on expired-conviction review and preserves custody as a statutory condition distinct from remedial usefulness. He would affirm the jurisdictional dismissal and therefore would not reach the marijuana claim’s merits. The Court rejects his sentence-order distinction for this continuous unexpired series; his dissent supplies no controlling limitation.

The named whole-opinion joins establish each Justice’s published position at the precise scope of the controlling holdings. Separate positions above remain noncontrolling and are retained for future continuity without becoming current doctrine. No later historical predicate is expressly displaced beyond the issues stated; no unsupported consequence index entry is created.

### Nebraska v. Wyoming; No. 108, Original. - 1995-05-30

Authority: [Record](../records/Nebraska_v_Wyoming_original_exceptions_1995-05-30.md#public-projection)

The decree's 75/25 natural-flow allocation cannot be replaced through these amendments by a mass beneficial-use ceiling. Reject the First Counterclaim and First Cross-Claim insofar as they seek that ceiling, while preserving discrete geographic, priority, unlawful-call and relevant waste defenses not dependent on it. Specific upstream groundwater development and alleged depletion threatening the decree allocation may be pleaded. Nebraska's own pumping creates no categorical unclean-hands bar without relevant conduct and connection to the asserted harm; supported defenses and applicable eventual proof requirements remain. Downstream nonirrigation and habitat effects may be considered insofar as relevant to injury and lawful modification in admitted decree claims. Relevance creates no independent instream entitlement, habitat-flow quantity, free-standing environmental right or automatic priority; Count IV remains denied without prejudice. Horse Creek's location below Tri-State Dam does not alone bar a specific changed-condition allegation threatening the decree's balance. A material decree connection and eventual modification proof remain required; no tributary-wide apportionment or finding of depletion follows. The Fourth Cross-Claim may proceed only for alleged changed federal storage administration materially affecting the interstate decree's operation or supporting conditions. Both United States and Nebraska exceptions are overruled within that scope; independent contractual compliance remains outside the original proceeding and in its lawful separate channel. Permit Nebraska Counts I-III and Wyoming Second-Fourth Counterclaims and Second-Fifth Cross-Claims within the preceding limits; deny Nebraska Count IV without prejudice and reject only impermissible ceiling portions of First Counterclaim and First Cross-Claim. Return Glendo, Laramie and carriage-loss theories to the Master for applicable proof; leave establishes no injury, breach, water award, priority, damages or injunction.

Original action remains open before Master Olpin for evidence and recommendations within admitted scope. Enforcement of an established right needs no fresh injury showing;modification requires clear and convincing real and substantial injury. CountIV remains denied without prejudice;all supported defenses remain and no water remedy arises from leave.

Published noncontrolling positions: Thomas concurs in all other components and dissents on the Fourth Cross-Claim. His objection concerns forum and scope,not an injury finding. Storage water was not apportioned,and he regards federal storage-contract administration as belonging to separate litigation. He would sustain both United States and Nebraska exceptions and exclude this pleading,preserving a later adequately distinct interstate modification claim. The majority's decree-effects limitation does not answer his alternative-forum objection. Federal party status and practical effects do not make independent administration appropriate for original adjudication. He finds no breach,immunity waiver or established contract relief and does not certify the separate forum's jurisdiction or complete remedy. His position is noncontrolling;the eight-Justice limited admission governs. No unpublished commitment changes current law.

### North Star Steel Co. v. Thomas / Crown Cork & Seal Co., Inc. v. United Steelworkers of America, AFL-CIO-CLC, Nos. 94-834 and 94-835 - 1995-05-30

Authority: [Record](../records/North_Star_Steel_v_Thomas_and_Crown_Cork_merits_1995-05-30.md#public-projection)

Scalia concurs in the judgment without other joins. He accepts state borrowing under existing precedent but does not endorse a judicial power to select a preferred federal period whenever Congress has omitted one. His distinct position supports both dispositions, not the Court opinion’s affirmative description of the narrow federal exception. He agrees that the asserted labor connection supplies no enacted six-month WARN period. His concurrence creates no alternative Court rule and does not choose the exact state statute.

### Reno v. Koray, No. 94-790 - 1995-06-05

Authority: [Record](../records/Reno_v_Koray_merits_1995-06-05.md#public-projection)

Ginsburg concurs while joining the Court’s opinion in full. She reserves whether an intelligent choice between bail and detention requires notice and comprehension of the consequences for sentence credit. That claim was not presented, so she decides no notice or due-process rule and supplies no new ground for statutory credit.

Stevens dissents without joins. He reads § 3585(b)’s term official detention in light of the restraint actually imposed, rather than treating related Bail Reform Act categories as an exclusive definition for all purposes. Crandon supports considering enacted words, context and purpose before any residual lenity. Court-ordered incarceration in an institution can be official even when a release caption is used, and Wilson allocates calculation without defining qualifying detention. The institutional-control theory does not authorize credit for ordinary curfews or every treatment placement. He would affirm the opportunity for lawful findings, not enter a credit award on allegations.

Stone-Zsela dissents without joins. He would hold that compulsory confinement in an institution under a court order can qualify when equivalent to incarceration, and that the conditional-release label alone cannot defeat the claim. The credit text does not say that only custody committed to the Attorney General counts; Wilson answers who initially computes credit, while Thomas Jefferson University concerns an agency’s own regulation and MCI confines administrative action to enacted authority. Those decisions do not make a program statement conclusive. Stone distinguishes official institutional incarceration from a free-floating hardship comparison. Ordinary home detention, curfew, work-release residence or voluntary rehabilitation is not categorically incarceration. Because the full conditions remain unproved, he would affirm a prompt bounded remand for actual compulsory conditions, the qualifying period and absence of duplicate credit, with the BOP’s computation conforming to § 3585(b) subject to lawful habeas review. He would not automatically award approximately 150 days, order release, revive equality, or replace the BOP as original calculator.
